\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))\big]

\displaystyle={\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{1}\leq V_{1},\ldots,W_{p}\leq V_{p}\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

(7.12)

For the second term in (
7.12
),

{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{k}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)=p{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big).

Proceeding as (
7.1
),

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{N}M\Big)=\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p(1-2M)}.

We then obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)=0.

(7.13)

For the first term in (
7.12
), notice that
[\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})]_{k=1}^{p}
are i.i.d. from
U(0,1)
since
[{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}]_{k=1}^{p}
are i.i.d. We then have

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}},\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}},\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})\leq V_{\max}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}\int_{0}^{1}pt^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}\int_{0}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\int_{1}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\Big].

For the first term,

\int_{0}^{1}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{1}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{1}{p}.

For the second term, using the Chernoff bound, conditional on
\widetilde{\bm{Z}}=({\widetilde{Z}}_{1},\ldots,{\widetilde{Z}}_{p})
,

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\,\Big|\,\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{p-1}\Big[\sum_{k=1}^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{k}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\,\Big|\,\widetilde{\bm{Z}}\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{d}}t=p\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{p-1}(1+t)^{M}\exp(-tM){\mathrm{d}}t=pe^{M}\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{M+p-1}\exp(-tM){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle pe^{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{M+p-1}\exp(-tM){\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{pe^{M}}{M^{M+p}}\Gamma(M+p)=\frac{pe^{M}}{M^{M+p}}(M+1)^{p-1}\Gamma(M+1)(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{pe^{M}}{M^{M+p}}(M+1)^{p-1}\sqrt{2\pi M}\frac{M^{M}}{e^{M}}(1+o(1))=\sqrt{2\pi}pM^{-1/2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{M}\Big)^{p-1}(1+o(1)),

where the last three steps are from Stirling’s approximation using
M\to\infty
.

Then

\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}p\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}\int_{1}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t=0,

and then we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}.

(7.14)

Plugging (
7.13
) and (
7.14
) into (
7.12
) yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))\big]\leq\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}=\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}r(x)\Big)^{p}.

(7.15)

Lastly, using Hölder’s inequality,

\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))\big]\geq\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]\Big]^{p}.

Employing the first claim, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))\big]\geq\big[r(x)\big]^{p}.

(7.16)

Combining (
7.15
) with (
7.16
) and noting that
\epsilon
is arbitrary, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]=\big[r(x)\big]^{p}.

(7.17)

Case II.2.

f_{1}(x)=0
. For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we take
\delta
in the same way as in the proof of Case I.2 and take
\eta_{N}
as in the proof of Case II.1.

By (
7.12
),

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))\big]\leq\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}>\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big).

For the first term,

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}},\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\lVert Z_{k}-x\rVert\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}f_{0}(x)\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}},\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\lVert Z_{k}-x\rVert\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}f_{0}(x)\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})\leq V_{\max}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}\int_{0}^{1}pt^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}f_{0}(x)t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{1}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}\int_{0}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}f_{0}(x)\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{1}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle+\int_{1}^{\frac{1-\epsilon}{\epsilon}f_{0}(x)\frac{N_{0}}{M}}t^{p-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V_{\max}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\,\Big|\,\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})=t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\Big].

Then proceeding in the same way as (
7.14
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max},V_{\max}\leq\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{1}{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{p}.

Lastly, using (
7.13
) and noting again that
\epsilon
is arbitrary, one obtains

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]=0=\big[r(x)\big]^{p}.

(7.18)

Combining (
7.17
) and (
7.18
) then completes the proof of the second claim.
∎

7.2
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Proof of Theorem
4.1
(i)
.

By (
2.4
) and that
[Z_{j}]_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
are i.i.d,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}}\frac{K_{M}(x)}{M}\Big]=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(Z_{j}\in A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big].

Employing Lemma
4.1
then completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
(ii)
.

By Hölder’s inequality, it suffices to consider the case when
p
is even. Noticing that
x^{p}
is convex for
p>1
and
x>0
, one has

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\rvert^{p}\big]\leq 2^{p-1}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]+{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]\Big).

(7.19)

For the second term in (
7.19
), Lemma
4.1
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]=0

(7.20)

by expanding the product term.

For the first term in (
7.19
), noticing that
[Z_{j}]_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
are i.i.d, we have
K_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}\sim{\rm Bin}(N_{1},\nu_{1}(A_{M}(x)))
. Using Lemma
4.1
and
MN_{1}/N_{0}\to\infty
, for any positive integers
p
and
q
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}[N_{1}^{p}\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))]=\big[r(x)\big]^{p},

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{p}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{q}{\mathrm{E}}[N_{1}^{p}\nu_{1}^{p+q}(A_{M}(x))]=\big[r(x)\big]^{p},

and then
{\mathrm{E}}[N_{1}^{p}\nu_{1}^{p}(A_{M}(x))]
is the dominated term among
[{\mathrm{E}}[N_{1}^{k}\nu_{1}^{k+q}(A_{M}(x))]]_{k\leq p,q\geq 0}
.

To complete the proof, for any positive integer
c
and
Z\sim{\rm Bin}(n,p^{\prime})
, let
\mu_{c}:={\mathrm{E}}[(Z-{\mathrm{E}}[Z])^{c}]
be the
c
-th central moment. According to
Romanovsky, 1923
, we have

\mu_{c+1}=p^{\prime}(1-p^{\prime})\Big(nc\mu_{c-1}+\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{c}}{{\mathrm{d}}p^{\prime}}\Big).

Then for even
p
, we obtain

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(K_{M}(x)-N_{1}\nu_{1}(A_{M}(x))\Big)^{p}\Big]\lesssim{\mathrm{E}}[N_{1}\nu_{1}(A_{M}(x))]^{p/2}\lesssim\Big(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{p/2}.

The first term in (
7.19
) then satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(K_{M}(x)-N_{1}\nu_{1}(A_{M}(x))\Big)^{p}\Big]\lesssim\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{p/2}.

Since
MN_{1}/N_{0}\to\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]=0.

(7.21)

Plugging (
7.20
) and (
7.21
) into (
7.19
) then completes the proof.
∎

7.3
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

We first cite the Hardy–Littlewood maximal inequality.

Lemma 7.2

(Hardy–Littlewood maximal inequality
(
Stein, 2016
)
)
.

For any locally integrable function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, define

{\sf M}f(x):=\sup_{\delta>0}\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z.

Then for
d\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C_{d}>0
only depending on
d
such that for all
t>0
and
f\in L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\lambda(\{x:{\sf M}f(x)>t\})<\frac{C_{d}}{t}\lVert f\rVert_{L_{1}},

where
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L_{1}}
stands for the function
L_{1}
norm.

Let
\epsilon>0
be given. We assume
\epsilon\leq f_{L}
. From Assumption
4.1
,
S_{0}
and
S_{1}
are bounded, then
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
are compactly supported. Since
f_{0},f_{1}\in L_{1}
and the class of continuous functions are dense in the class of compactly supported
L_{1}
functions from simple use of Lusin’s theorem, we can find
g_{0},g_{1}
such that
g_{0},g_{1}
are continuous and
\lVert f_{0}-g_{0}\rVert_{L_{1}}\leq\epsilon^{3}
and
\lVert f_{1}-g_{1}\rVert_{L_{1}}\leq\epsilon^{3}
.

Since
g_{0},g_{1}
are continuous with compact supports, they are uniformly continuous, that is, there exists
\delta>0
such that for any
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta
, we have

\lvert g_{0}(x)-g_{0}(z)\rvert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}~~~{\rm and}~~~\lvert g_{1}(x)-g_{1}(z)\rvert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(x)-f_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(x)-g_{0}(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z+\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert g_{0}(x)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z+\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(z)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lvert f_{0}(x)-g_{0}(x)\rvert+\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert g_{0}(x)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z+\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(z)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z.

(7.22)

For the first term in (
7.22
), using Markov’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\lambda\Big(\Big\{x:\Big\lvert f_{0}(x)-g_{0}(x)\Big\rvert>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}\Big)\leq\frac{3}{\epsilon^{2}}\lVert f_{0}-g_{0}\rVert_{L_{1}}\leq 3\epsilon.

(7.23)

For the second term in (
7.22
), by the selection of
\delta
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert g_{0}(x)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\max_{z\in B_{x,\delta}}\lvert g_{0}(x)-g_{0}(z)\rvert\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}.

(7.24)

For the third term,

\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(z)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\sup_{\delta>0}\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(z)-g_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z={\sf M}(f_{0}-g_{0})(x).

Lemma
7.2
then yields

\displaystyle\lambda\Big(\Big\{x:{\sf M}(f_{0}-g_{0})(x)>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}\Big)<\frac{3C_{d}}{\epsilon^{2}}\lVert f_{0}-g_{0}\rVert_{L_{1}}\leq 3C_{d}\epsilon.

(7.25)

We can establish similar results for
f_{1},g_{1}
.

Let

\displaystyle A_{1}:=

\displaystyle\Big\{x:\Big\lvert f_{0}(x)-g_{0}(x)\Big\rvert>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}\bigcup\Big\{x:\Big\lvert f_{1}(x)-g_{1}(x)\Big\rvert>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}\bigcup

\displaystyle\Big\{x:{\sf M}(f_{0}-g_{0})(x)>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}\bigcup\Big\{x:{\sf M}(f_{1}-g_{1})(x)>\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{3}\Big\}.

Plugging (
7.23
), (
7.24
), (
7.25
) into (
7.22
), for any
x\notin A_{1}
and
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(x)-f_{0}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\epsilon^{2},~~\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(z)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\epsilon^{2},

and

\lambda(A_{1})\leq 6(C_{d}+1)\epsilon.

Let
A_{2}:=\Big\{x:f_{1}(x)\leq\epsilon\Big\}
. We then seperate the proof into three cases. In the following, it suffices to consider
f_{0}(x)>0
due to the definition of
L_{p}
risk in (
4.1
).

Case I.

x\notin A_{1}\cup A_{2}
.

According to
\epsilon\leq f_{L}
and by the definition of
A_{2}
, for any
x\notin A_{1}\cup A_{2}
and
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{0}(x)-f_{0}(y)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\epsilon^{2}\leq\epsilon f_{L}\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\delta})}\int_{B_{x,\delta}}\lvert f_{1}(x)-f_{1}(y)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq\epsilon^{2}\leq\epsilon f_{1}(x).

We then obtain

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{0}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x),~~\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{0}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x),

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{1}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{1}(x),~~\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{1}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{1}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{1}(x).

Let
\eta_{N}=\eta_{N,p}=4p\log(N_{0}/M)
. We also take
N_{0}
large enough so that

\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}=4p\frac{M}{N_{0}}\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)<(1-\epsilon)f_{L}\lambda(B_{0,\delta}).

Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
f_{0}(x)>0
, we have

\eta_{N}\frac{M}{N_{0}}<(1-\epsilon)f_{0}(x)\lambda(B_{0,\delta}).

Proceeding as in the proof of Case II.1 of Lemma
4.1
and also Theorem
4.1
by using Fubini’s theorem, since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\notin A_{1}\cup A_{2}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]=0.

(7.26)

Case II.

x\in A_{2}\setminus A_{1}
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{0}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x),~~\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{0}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x),

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{1}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon^{2},~~\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{1}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{1}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\epsilon^{2}.

Take
\eta_{N}
and take
N_{0}
sufficiently large as in Case I above. Proceeding as the proof of Case II.2 of Lemma
4.1
and also Theorem
4.1
by using Fubini’s theorem, since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in A_{2}\setminus A_{1}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]=0.

(7.27)

Case III.

x\in A_{1}
.

In this case, for any
x\in A_{1}
and
z\in S_{1}
,

\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq f_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}\cap S_{0})\geq af_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq\frac{af_{L}}{f_{U}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert}).

Then for any
x\in A_{1}
, from (
7.12
) and in the same way as (
7.14
),

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}^{p}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]=\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{1}\leq V_{1},\ldots,W_{p}\leq V_{p}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W_{\max}\leq V_{\max}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{p}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{af_{L}}{f_{U}}\max_{k\in\llbracket p\rrbracket}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert{\widetilde{Z}}_{k}-x\rVert})\leq V_{\max}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{af_{L}}\Big)^{p}(1+o(1))=O(1).

Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, and due to the boundedness assumptions on
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
, for any
x\in A_{1}
and
p
even,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]\lesssim{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]+{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)^{p}\Big]+\Big\lvert r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}\lesssim 1.

Then

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in A_{1}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]\lesssim f_{U}\lambda(A_{1})\lesssim\epsilon.

Since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, one has

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in A_{1}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]=0.

(7.28)

Combining (
7.26
), (
7.27
), and (
7.28
) completes the proof.
∎

7.4
Proof of Theorem
4.3

We only have to prove the first two claims as the rest are trivial.

Proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta/2
, since
B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}\subset B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert}\subset B_{x,\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{0}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f_{0}(y)-f_{0}(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y\leq 2L\lVert z-x\rVert,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f_{1}(x)\Big\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}z\leq L\lVert z-x\rVert.

Consider any
\delta_{N}>0
such that
\delta_{N}\leq\delta/2
. If
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
and
f_{0}(x)>2L\delta_{N}
, then

\frac{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}\leq\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})}\frac{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert x-z\rVert})}{\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-z\rVert})}.

If further
f_{1}(x)>L\delta_{N}
, then

\frac{\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})}\frac{\lambda(B_{x,\lVert x-z\rVert})}{\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-z\rVert})}\leq\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}.

On the other hand, if
\lVert z-x\rVert\geq\delta_{N}
and
f_{0}(x)>2L\delta_{N}
,

\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq(f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N})\lambda(B_{0,\delta_{N}})=(f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N})V_{d}\delta_{N}^{d},

where
V_{d}
is the Lebesgue measure of the unit ball on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Let
\delta_{N}=(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
. Since
M/N_{0}\to 0
, we have
\delta_{N}\to 0
as
N_{0}\to\infty
. Taking
N_{0}
large enough so that
\delta_{N}<f_{L}/(4L)
and
\delta_{N}\leq\delta/2
, then

2LV_{d}\delta_{N}^{d+1}=\frac{M}{N_{0}}\frac{8L}{f_{L}}\delta_{N}<2\frac{M}{N_{0}}.

Then for any
(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{x,\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},L,d,\delta)
,

(f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N})V_{d}\delta_{N}^{d}>4\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{L}}\frac{M}{N_{0}}-2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\geq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}.

With a slight abuse of notation, let
W:=\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert x-Z\rVert})
. Then
W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}
implies that
\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
.

Depending on the value of
f_{1}(x)
, the proof is separated into two cases.

Case I.

f_{1}(x)>L\delta_{N}
.

Upper bound.
Proceeding similar to (
7.3
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}U\leq U_{(M)}\Big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big).

(7.29)

For the second term in (
7.29
), since
M/\log N_{0}\to\infty
, for any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\rm Bin}\Big(N_{0},2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq M\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}\exp\Big((\log 2-1)M\Big)=\frac{N_{0}}{M}N_{0}^{-(1-\log 2)M/\log N_{0}}\prec N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

(7.30)

For the first term in (
7.29
), proceeding as (
7.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}U\leq U_{(M)}\Big)\leq\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]\leq\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma}).

(7.31)

Lower bound.
Proceeding similar to (
7.8
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}U\leq U_{(M)}\Big)-\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\int_{0}^{\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t-\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big).

Consider the first term. If
\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\int_{0}^{\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}.

If
\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}<1
, using the Chernoff bound, for any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}^{\frac{N_{0}}{M}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}^{\frac{N_{0}}{M}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}}^{\frac{N_{0}}{M}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}t\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big[1-\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}\Big]\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}\Big)\leq\Big[1-\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}\Big]\frac{N_{0}}{M}\exp\Big[M-\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}N_{0}-M\log M+M\log\Big(\frac{f_{L}}{f_{U}}N_{0}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

The last step is due to
M\log N_{0}/N_{0}\to 0
. Recalling (
7.4
), we then obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]\geq\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}-o(N_{0}^{-\gamma}).

(7.32)

Combining (
7.31
) and (
7.32
), and taking
N_{0}
large enough so that
L\delta_{N}\leq f_{U}\wedge(f_{L}/4)
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]-r(x)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big\lvert\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}-\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big\rvert\bigvee\Big\lvert\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}-\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big\rvert+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{f_{0}(x)L\delta_{N}+2f_{1}(x)L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)(f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N})}+\frac{1}{N_{0}+1}\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{2}{f_{L}}+\frac{4f_{U}}{f_{L}^{2}}\Big)L\delta_{N}+\frac{4f_{U}}{f_{L}}\frac{1}{N_{0}+1}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma}).

By the selection of
\delta_{N}
and that the right hand side does not depend on
x
, we complete the proof for this case.

Case II.

f_{1}(x)\leq L\delta_{N}
.

The upper bound (
7.31
) in Case I still holds for this case. Accordingly, taking
N_{0}
large enough so that
L\delta_{N}\leq f_{L}/4
, one has

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]-r(x)\Big\rvert\leq{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]+r(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}+\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma})\leq\frac{4}{f_{L}}L\delta_{N}+\frac{1}{f_{L}}L\delta_{N}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma}).

We thus complete the whole proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
2
.

By the law of total variance,

\displaystyle\Var\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]+\Var\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big].

(7.33)

For the first term in (
7.33
), let
Z
be a copy drawn from
\nu_{1}
independently of the data. Then, since
[Z_{j}]_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
are i.i.d,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}K_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(Z_{j}\in A_{M}(x)\big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]=\frac{N_{0}^{2}}{N_{1}M^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\mathds{1}\big(Z\in A_{M}(x)\big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}^{2}}{N_{1}M^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)-\nu_{1}^{2}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]\leq\frac{N_{0}^{2}}{N_{1}M^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]\lesssim C\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M},

(7.34)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
. The last step is due to (
7.31
).

For the second term in (
7.33
), notice that

\displaystyle\Var\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]=\Var\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}K_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]=\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Var\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big].

Recalling that
W=\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert x-Z\rVert})
, we have the following lemma about the density of
W
near
x
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Denote the density of
W
by
f_{W}
. Then for any
(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{x,\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},L,d,\delta)
,

f_{W}(0)=r(x).

Furthermore, for any
\epsilon>0
and
N_{0}
sufficiently large, we have for all
0\leq w\leq 2M/N_{0}
,

\sup_{(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},\delta,L,d)}f_{W}(w)\leq(1+\epsilon)\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}.

Due to Lemma
7.3
, we can take
N_{0}
sufficiently large so that for any
0\leq w\leq 2M/N_{0}
,

\sup_{(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},\delta,L,d)}f_{W}(w)\leq 2\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}.

Let
Z,{\widetilde{Z}}
be two independent copies from
\nu_{1}
that are further independent of the data. Let
W=\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert x-Z\rVert})
and
{\widetilde{W}}=\nu_{0}(B_{{\widetilde{Z}},\lVert x-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert})
. Let
V=\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})
and
{\widetilde{V}}=\nu_{0}(B_{{\widetilde{Z}},\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}({\widetilde{Z}})-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert})
. We then have

\displaystyle\Var\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}^{2}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]-\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(Z\in A_{M}(x),{\widetilde{Z}}\in A_{M}(x)\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(Z\in A_{M}(x)\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{Z}}\in A_{M}(x)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert,\lVert x-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}({\widetilde{Z}})-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big(\lVert x-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}({\widetilde{Z}})-{\widetilde{Z}}\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big).

Due to the independence between
Z
and
{\widetilde{Z}}
,
W
and
{\widetilde{W}}
are independent. Notice that
V\,|\,Z
have the same distribution as
U_{(M)}
for any
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, then
V
and
Z
are independent, so are
{\widetilde{V}}
and
{\widetilde{Z}}
.

Let’s expand the variance further as

\displaystyle\Var\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big].

(7.35)

For the first term in (
7.35
), we have the following lemma.

Lemma 7.4
.

We have

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{M},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

For the second term in (
7.35
),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big).

Using the Chernoff bound and
M/\log N_{0}\to\infty
, for any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\exp\Big[-(1-\log 2)M\Big]\prec N_{0}^{-\gamma},

We then have

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\prec N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

(7.36)

For the third term in (
7.35
), it is easy to see

\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]\geq 0.

Plugging Lemma
7.4
and (
7.36
) into (
7.35
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Var\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\Big]\lesssim C\frac{1}{M},

(7.37)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Plugging (
7.34
) and (
7.37
) into (
7.33
) completes the proof.
∎

7.5
Proof of Theorem
4.4

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

We only have to prove the first claim as the second is trivial.

Take
\delta_{N}=(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
as in the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
. Take
\delta_{N}^{\prime}=(\frac{2}{af_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, denote the distance of
x
to the boundary of
S_{1}
by
\Delta(x)
, i.e.,
\Delta(x)=\inf_{z\in\partial S_{1}}\lVert z-x\rVert
.

Depending on the position of
x
and the value of
\Delta(x)
, we separate the proof into three cases .

Case I.

x\in S_{1}
and
\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}
.

In this case, since
\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}
, for any
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
, we have
B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}\subset S_{1}
. From the smoothness conditions on
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
, similar to the proof of Theorem
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in S_{1},\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert\Big]f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in S_{1},\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big]^{2}\Big)^{1/2}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in S_{1},\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d}+\Big(\frac{1}{M}\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{MN_{1}}\Big)^{1/2}\Big]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in S_{1},\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d}+\Big(\frac{1}{M}\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{MN_{1}}\Big)^{1/2}\Big]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\Big[\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d}+\Big(\frac{1}{M}\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{MN_{1}}\Big)^{1/2}\Big],

(7.38)

where the constant
C>0
only depends on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d
.

Case II.

x\in S_{0}\setminus S_{1}
and
\Delta(x)>\delta_{N}^{\prime}
.

In this case,
r(x)=0
and for any
z\in S_{1}
,

\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq f_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}\cap S_{0})\geq af_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})>af_{L}V_{d}\delta_{N}^{\prime d}\geq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}.

Then for any
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert\Big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\Big]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{E}}\big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\Big)\leq\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}>2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\prec N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

One then obtains

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\notin S_{1},\Delta(x)>\delta_{N}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle N_{0}^{-\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(x\in S_{0}\setminus S_{1},\Delta(x)>\delta_{N}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\leq N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

(7.39)

Case III.

x\in S_{0}
and
\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}
.

In this case, for any
z\in S_{1}
,

\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq f_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}\cap S_{0})\geq af_{L}\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})\geq\frac{af_{L}}{f_{U}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert z-x\rVert}).

Accordingly,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert\Big]\leq{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\Big]+r(x)=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\Big)+r(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{af_{L}}{f_{U}}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert}),\nu_{0}(B_{Z,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z)-Z\rVert})\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)+r(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{af_{L}}{f_{U}}U\leq U_{(M)}\Big)+r(x)=\frac{f_{U}}{af_{L}}(1+o(1))+\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}.

From the definition of
\delta_{N},\delta_{N}^{\prime}
and
M/N_{0}\to 0
, we have
\delta_{N},\delta_{N}^{\prime}\to 0
as
N_{0}\to\infty
. Since the surface area of
S_{1}
is bounded by
H
, we have

\lambda(\{x:\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}\})\lesssim H\{(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}\}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(x)-r(x)\Big\rvert\Big]f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{af_{L}}(1+o(1))+\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{0}(x)\mathds{1}(\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}){\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{af_{L}}(1+o(1))+\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)f_{U}\lambda(\{x:\Delta(x)\leq(2\delta_{N})\vee\delta_{N}^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{af_{L}}+\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)f_{U}H(\delta_{N}+\delta_{N}^{\prime})\leq C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d},

(7.40)

where the constant
C>0
only depends on
f_{L},f_{U},a,H,d
.

Combining (
7.38
), (
7.39
), (
7.40
) completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of the rest results

A.1
Proofs of the results in Section
3

A.1.1
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

We consider the complexities of two algorithms seperately.

Algorithm
1
.

The worst-case computation complexity of building a balanced
k
-d tree is
O(dN_{0}\log N_{0})
(cf.
Brown, 2015
) since the size of the k-d tree is
N_{0}
.

The average computation complexity of searching a NN is
O(\log N_{0})
from
Friedman et al., 1977
, and then the average computation complexity of search
M
-NNs in
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
for all
\{Z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
is
O(MN_{1}\log N_{0})
.

Notice that
\lvert S_{j}\rvert=M
for any
j\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
and then
\big\lvert\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S_{j}\big\rvert\leq N_{1}M
. Since the elements of each
S_{j}
are in
\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
, the largest integer in
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S_{j}
is
N_{0}
. Then the computation complexity of counting step is
O(N_{1}M+N_{0})
due to the counting sort algorithm
(
Cormen et al., 2009
, Section 8.2)
.

Combining the above three steps completes the proof for Algorithm
1
.

Algorithm
2
.

The computation complexity of building a
k
-d tree is
O(d(N_{0}+n)\log(N_{0}+n))
from Algorithm
1
since the size of the
k
-d tree is
N_{0}+n
.

For the searching step, for each
j\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
, the number of NNs to be searched is

M+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\big(\lVert x_{i}-Z_{j}\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z_{j})-Z_{j}\rVert\big).

Then from (
2.2
), the total number of NNs searched for all
j\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
is

\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\Big(M+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}\big(\lVert x_{i}-Z_{j}\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Z_{j})-Z_{j}\rVert\big)\Big)=N_{1}M+\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{M}(x_{i}).

Let
X,Y
be two independent copies from
\nu_{0},\nu_{1}
, respectively. Since
[Z_{j}]_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
are i.i.d. and
[X_{i}]_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\bigcup[x_{i}]_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d, we have

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{M}(x_{i})\Big]=n{\mathrm{E}}[K_{M}(X)]=N_{1}n{\mathrm{E}}[\nu_{1}(A_{M}(X))]=N_{1}n\frac{M}{N_{0}+1},

where the last equation is due to

{\mathrm{E}}[\nu_{1}(A_{M}(X))]={\mathrm{P}}\Big(\lVert X-Y\rVert\leq\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(Y)-Y\rVert\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(U\leq U_{(M)}\Big)=\frac{M}{N_{0}+1},

by using the probability integral transform. Then the average computation complexity for the searching step is
O(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}(N_{0}+n)\log(N_{0}+n))

For the counting step, the computation complexity for counting
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S_{j}
is
O(N_{0}+N_{1}M)
since the cardinality of
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S_{j}
is
N_{1}M
and the largest integer is
N_{0}
. The average computation complexity for counting
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S^{\prime}_{j}
is
O(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}n+n)
since the average cardinality of
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}S^{\prime}_{j}
is
\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}n
and the largest integer is
n
.

Combining the above three steps completes the proof for Algorithm
2
.
∎

A.2
Proofs of the results in Section
4

A.2.1
Proof of Corollary
4.1

Proof of Corollary
4.1
.

Corollary
4.1
can be established following the same way as that of Theorem
4.2
but with less effort since we only have to show

\lim_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]=0.

In detail, denote the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the probability measure of
W
with respect to
\nu_{0}
by
r_{W}
. We then have

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(W)\big)-r(W)\Big\rvert^{p}\Big]=\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}r_{W}(x){\mathrm{d}}\nu_{0}(x)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}r_{W}(x)f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\limsup_{N_{0}\to\infty}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]-r(x)\Big\rvert^{p}f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\Big]=0.

Here the last line has intrinsically been established in the proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Noticing that
{\mathrm{E}}\big[r(W)\big]^{p}
is bounded under Assumption
4.1
, the proof is thus complete.
∎

A.2.2
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

We take
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
to share the same support, and assume
x
to be the origin of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
without loss of generality.

When
N_{1}\lesssim N_{0}
, we take
\nu_{0}
to be the uniform distribution with density
f_{L}
on
[-f_{L}^{-1/d}/2,f_{L}^{-1/d}/2]^{d}
. Then the MSE is lower bounded by the density estimation over Lipchitz class with
N_{1}
samples.

When
N_{0}\lesssim N_{1}
, we take
\nu_{1}
to be the uniform distribution with density
f_{U}
on
[-f_{U}^{-1/d}/2,f_{U}^{-1/d}/2]^{d}
. Notice that
1/f_{0}
is also local Lipchitz from the lower boundness condition and local Lipchitz condition on
f_{0}
. Then the MSE is lower bounded by the density estimation over Lipchitz class with
N_{0}
samples.

We then complete the proof by combining the above two lower bounds and then using the famous minimax lower bound in Lipschitz density estimation
(
Tsybakov, 2009
, Exercise 2.8)
,
∎

A.2.3
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

We take
\nu_{0}
and
\nu_{1}
to be of the same support.

When
N_{1}\lesssim N_{0}
, we take
\nu_{0}
to be the uniform distribution with density
f_{L}
on
[-f_{L}^{-1/d}/2,f_{L}^{-1/d}/2]^{d}
. Then the
L_{1}
risk is lower bounded by the
L_{1}
risk over support of density estimation over Lipchitz class with
N_{1}
samples.

When
N_{0}\lesssim N_{1}
, we take
\nu_{1}
to be the uniform distribution with density
f_{U}
on
[-f_{U}^{-1/d}/2,f_{U}^{-1/d}/2]^{d}
. Notice
1/f_{0}
is also Lipchitz from the lower boundness condition and Lipchitz condition on
f_{0}
. From the lower boundness condition on
f_{0}
, the
L_{1}
risk is lower bounded by the
L_{1}
risk over support of density estimation over Lipchitz class with
N_{0}
samples.

We then complete the proof by combining the above two lower bounds and then using then the minimax lower bound of
L_{1}
risk for density estimation over Lipchitz class
(
Zhao and Lai, 2020a
, Theorem 1)
.
∎

A.3
Proofs of the results in Section
5

We first formally define
\widetilde{\tau}_{M,K}^{\rm bc}
. Assume
n
is divisible by
K
for simplicity. Let
[I_{k}]_{k=1}^{K}
be a
K
-fold random partition of
\llbracket n\rrbracket
, with each of size equal to
n^{\prime}=n/K
. For each
k\in\llbracket K\rrbracket
and
\omega\in\{0,1\}
, construct
K_{M,k}^{\omega}(\cdot),\widehat{\mu}_{\omega,k}(\cdot)
using data
[(X_{i},D_{i},Y_{i})]_{i=1,i\notin I_{k}}^{n}
. Then define

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.81944pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.81944pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.01389pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.68787pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.53241pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.53241pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=2.15277pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.40804pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}}^{\rm bc}_{M,k}=\frac{1}{n^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1,i\in I_{k}}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1,k}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0,k}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{\prime}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\in I_{k},D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M,k}(X_{i})}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{1,k}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1,i\in I_{k},D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M,k}(X_{i})}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{0,k}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{M,K}^{\rm bc}:=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.81944pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.81944pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.01389pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.68787pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.53241pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.53241pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=2.15277pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.40804pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}}^{\rm bc}_{M,k}.

A.3.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

From Equ.(3) in
Abadie and Imbens, 2011
, the bias-corrected estimator imputes the missing potential outcomes by

\displaystyle\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(0):=\begin{cases}Y_{i},&\mbox{ if }D_{i}=0,\\
\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{0}_{M}(i)}(Y_{j}+\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{j})),&\mbox{ if }D_{i}=1,\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(1):=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{1}_{M}(i)}(Y_{j}+\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{j})),&\mbox{ if }D_{i}=0,\\
Y_{i},&\mbox{ if }D_{i}=1.\end{cases}

The bias-corrected estimator is defined as:

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm bc}_{M}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(1)-\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(0)\Big].

Then by simple algebra, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm bc}_{M}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(1)-\widehat{Y}^{\rm bc}_{i}(0)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big[Y_{i}-\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{0}_{M}(i)}(Y_{j}+\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{j}))\Big]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big[\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{1}_{M}(i)}(Y_{j}+\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{j}))-Y_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{R}_{i}+\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{0}_{M}(i)}\widehat{R}_{j}\Big]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big[\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{1}_{M}(i)}\widehat{R}_{j}-\widehat{R}_{i}+\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big].

This completes the proof.
∎

A.3.2
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Proof of Theorem
5.1
(i)
.

Part I.
Suppose the density function is sufficiently smooth.
For any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
, let
\bar{R}_{i}:=Y_{i}-\bar{\mu}_{D_{i}}(X_{i})
. From (
5.2
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm reg}+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm reg}+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}Y_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}Y_{i}\Big].

(A.1)

For each term, we only establish the first half part, and the second half can be established in the same way.

For the first term in (
A.1
),

\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big]\Big\rvert\leq\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\bar{\mu}_{1}\rVert_{\infty}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.2)

For the second term in (
A.1
),

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\bar{\mu}_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)=\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\bar{\mu}_{1}\rVert_{\infty}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1),

where the last step is due to
\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}K^{1}_{M}(i)=n_{0}M
. We then have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.3)

For the third term in (
A.1
), by Theorem
4.2
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}\Big\rvert\Big]\leq{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}\Big\rvert\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big]^{2}{\mathrm{E}}[D_{i}\bar{R}_{i}]^{2}={\mathrm{E}}\Big[1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big]^{2}{\mathrm{E}}[D_{i}(Y_{i}(1)-\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big]^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})+(\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i}))^{2}\Big]=o(1).

We then obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{R}_{i}\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.4)

For the fourth term in (
A.1
), notice that

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]=0.

Then

\displaystyle\Var\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\bar{\mu}^{2}_{1}(X_{i})\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}-1\Big)\Big]=O(n^{-1}).

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)\bar{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.5)

For the fifth term in (
A.1
), notice that
{\mathrm{E}}[Y^{2}]
are bounded and
[(X_{i},D_{i},Y_{i})]_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d.. Using the weak law of large numbers
(
Durrett, 2019
, Theorem 2.2.3)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}Y_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}Y_{i}\Big]\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[Y_{i}(1)-Y_{i}(0)\Big]=\tau.

(A.6)

Plugging (
A.2
), (
A.3
), (
A.4
), (
A.5
), (
A.6
) into (
A.1
) completes the proof.

Part II.
Suppose the outcome model is correct.
By (
5.2
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm reg}+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm reg}+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\mu_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big].

(A.7)

For the first term in (
A.7
),

\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big]\Big\rvert\leq\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.8)

For the second term in (
A.7
),

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)=\lVert\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1),

where the last step is due to
\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}K^{1}_{M}(i)=n_{0}M
. We then have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(\mu_{0}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.9)

For the third term in (
A.7
), noticing that
K_{M}^{1}(\cdot)
is a function of
\bm{X}
and
\bm{D}
, one could obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]=0.

Accordingly, we have

\displaystyle\Var\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})\Big]\leq\frac{1}{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big]\lVert\sigma_{1}^{2}\rVert_{\infty},

where
\sigma_{1}^{2}(x)={\mathrm{E}}[U^{2}_{1}\,|\,X=x]
for
x\in\mathbb{X}
.

Conditional on
\bm{D}
, for any
j,\ell\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
and
j\neq\ell
such that
D_{j}=D_{\ell}=0
, for
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
such that
D_{i}=1
, it is easy to check
{\mathrm{E}}[K_{M}^{1}(i)\,|\,\bm{D}]=Mn_{0}/n_{1}
and
{\mathrm{E}}[(K_{M}^{1}(i))^{2}\,|\,\bm{D}]\asymp M^{2}
. Then

\Var\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]=O(n^{-1}).

We then obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i})\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

(A.10)

For the fourth term in (
A.7
), notice that
{\mathrm{E}}[\mu_{\omega}^{2}]
is bounded for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
. Using the weak law of large number, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big]\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big]=\tau.

(A.11)

Plugging (
A.8
), (
A.9
), (
A.10
), (
A.11
) into (
A.7
) completes the proof.

Analogous analysis can be performed on
{\mathchoice{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\displaystyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 6.25pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 6.25pt\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=4.30554pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=4.37154pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\textstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.81944pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.81944pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=3.01389pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.68787pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}{{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hbox{\raise 5.53241pt\hbox{\scalebox{1}[-1]{\lower 5.53241pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\vrule width=0.0pt,height=2.15277pt\vrule height=0.0pt,width=3.40804pt}$}}}}\cr\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tau$}\crcr}}}}}^{\rm bc}_{M,k}
for any
k\in\llbracket K\rrbracket
. Then the results apply to
\widetilde{\tau}_{M,K}^{\rm bc}
automatically since
K
is fixed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
(ii)
.

From Definition 3.1 in
Chernozhukov et al., 2018
,
\widetilde{\tau}_{M,K}^{\rm bc}
is the double machine learning estimator. We then follow the proof of Theorem 5.1 (recalling Remark
5.3
) and use the notations in
Chernozhukov et al., 2018
, essentially checking Assumption 3.1 and 3.2 therein. In the following the notation in
Chernozhukov et al., 2018
is adopted.

For estimating the ATE, from Equ. (5.3) in
Chernozhukov et al., 2018
, the score is

\psi(X,D,Y;\widetilde{\tau},\widetilde{\zeta}):=\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)+\frac{D(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X))}{\widetilde{e}(X)}-\frac{(1-D)(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X))}{1-\widetilde{e}(X)}-\widetilde{\tau},

where
\widetilde{\zeta}(x)=(\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(x),\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(x),\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(x),\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(x))
are the nuisance parameters by letting
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(x)=1/(1-\widetilde{e}(x))
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(x)=1/\widetilde{e}(x)
. Let
\rho_{0}(x)=1/(1-e(x))
and
\rho_{1}(x)=1/e(x)
. Then the true value is
\zeta(x)=(\mu_{0}(x),\mu_{1}(x),\rho_{0}(x),\rho_{1}(x))
.

We can then write the score as

\psi(X,D,Y;\widetilde{\tau},\widetilde{\zeta})=\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)+D(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)-(1-D)(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)-\widetilde{\tau}.

For any
p>0
, let
\lVert f\rVert_{p}:=\lVert f(X,D,Y)\rVert_{p}=(\int\lvert f(\omega)\rvert^{p}{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{P}}_{(X,D,Y)}(\omega))^{1/p}
. For the
\kappa
in Assumption
5.1
, let
q=2+\kappa/2
,
q_{1}=2+\kappa
and
q_{2}
such that
q^{-1}=q_{1}^{-1}+q_{2}^{-1}
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{n}
be the set consisting of all
\widetilde{\zeta}
such that for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
,

\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{\omega}-\mu_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}=o(n^{-d/(4+2d)}),~~\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{\omega}-\rho_{\omega}\rVert_{1}=O(n^{-1/(d+2)}),~~\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{\omega}-\rho_{\omega}\rVert_{q_{2}}=o(1).

Then the selection of
\mathcal{T}_{n}
satisfies Assumption 3.2(a) in
Chernozhukov et al., 2018
from Assumption
5.5
, Theorem
4.4
, and Theorem
4.2
, respectively.

For step 1, we verify the Neyman orthogonality. It is easy to see
{\mathrm{E}}\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\zeta)=0
. For any
\widetilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}
, the Gateaux derivative in the direction
\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta
is

\displaystyle\partial_{\widetilde{\zeta}}{\mathrm{E}}\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\zeta)[\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta]={\mathrm{E}}[\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\mu_{1}(X)]-{\mathrm{E}}[\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)-\mu_{0}(X)]

\displaystyle-{\mathrm{E}}[D(\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\mu_{1}(X))\rho_{1}(X)]+{\mathrm{E}}[(1-D)(\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)-\mu_{0}(X))\rho_{0}(X)]

\displaystyle+{\mathrm{E}}[D(Y-\mu_{1}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)-\rho_{1}(X))]-{\mathrm{E}}[(1-D)(Y-\mu_{0}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)-\rho_{0}(X))].

It is easy to check the above quantity is zero, which completes this step.

Step 2 and step 3 can be directly applied.

For step 4, we can establish in the same way that for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
,
\lVert\mu_{\omega}\rVert_{q_{1}}=O(1)
from
\lVert Y\rVert_{q_{1}}=O(1)
, and
\tau=O(1)
. Then from Hölder’s inequality and
\lVert\rho_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}
is bounded for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\widetilde{\zeta})\lVert_{q}=\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)+D(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)-(1-D)(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)-\tau\rVert_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)\rVert_{q}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)\rVert_{q}+\lVert(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)\rVert_{q}+\lVert(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)\rVert_{q}+\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\mu_{1}\rVert_{q}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert\mu_{0}\rVert_{q}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{\infty}+(\lVert Y\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\mu_{1}\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty})\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{1}\rVert_{q_{2}}

\displaystyle+(\lVert Y\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\mu_{0}\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{\infty})\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\rVert_{q_{2}}+\tau=O(1).

The last step is from the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{n}
. Then we completes this step.

For step 5, from Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\lVert\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\widetilde{\zeta})-\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\zeta)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{2}+\lVert D(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)-D(Y-\mu_{1}(X))\rho_{1}(X)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle+\lVert(1-D)(Y-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X))\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)-(1-D)(Y-\mu_{0}(X))\rho_{0}(X)\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{2}+\lVert(Y-\mu_{1}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}-\rho_{1})\rVert_{2}+\lVert(\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1})\widetilde{\rho}_{1}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle+\lVert(Y-\mu_{0}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{0}-\rho_{0})\rVert_{2}+\lVert(\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0})\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{\infty}+(\lVert Y\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\mu_{1}(X)\rVert_{q_{1}})\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{1}-\rho_{1}\rVert_{q_{2}}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{1}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle+(\lVert Y\rVert_{q_{1}}+\lVert\mu_{0}(X)\rVert_{q_{1}})\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{q_{2}}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{0}\rVert_{2}=o(1).

The last step is due to the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{n}
.

Notice that for any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\zeta+t(\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Big({\mathrm{E}}[D(\widetilde{\mu}_{1}(X)-\mu_{1}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}(X)-\rho_{1}(X))]-{\mathrm{E}}[(1-D)(\widetilde{\mu}_{0}(X)-\mu_{0}(X))(\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(X)-\rho_{0}(X))]\Big).

Then from the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{n}
,

\lvert\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\psi(X,D,Y;\tau,\zeta+t(\widetilde{\zeta}-\zeta))\rvert\leq 2[\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{1}-\rho_{1}\rVert_{1}+\lVert\widetilde{\mu}_{0}-\mu_{0}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert\widetilde{\rho}_{0}-\rho_{0}\rVert_{1}]=o(n^{-1/2}).

We then complete this step and thus complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
(ii)
, consistency of
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}
.

We decompose
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\sigma^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})+D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})+D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+D_{i}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-(1-D_{i})\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\tau\Big]^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})+\frac{D_{i}}{e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{i}))-\frac{1-D_{i}}{1-e(X_{i})}(Y_{i}-\mu_{0}(X_{i}))-\tau\Big]^{2}-\sigma^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}.

By Assumption
5.4
, Assumption
5.1
, Theorem
4.2
, as well as the fact that
\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, we have
T_{1}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
. By Assumption
5.1
, Theorem
4.2
, and
\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}=O_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, we have
T_{2}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
. By Assumption
5.1
and
\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}-\tau=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
, we have
T_{3}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
. By the fact that
[(X_{i},D_{i},Y_{i})]_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d., Assumption
5.1
and the weak law of large numbers, we have
T_{4}=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1)
. Combining the above four facts together then completes the proof.
∎

A.3.3
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

For
\omega\in\{0,1\}
, let
j^{\omega}_{M}(i)
represent the index of
M
th-NN of
X_{i}
in
\{X_{j}:D_{j}=\omega\}_{j=1}^{n}
, i.e., the index
j\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
such that
D_{j}=\omega
and

\sum_{\ell=1,D_{\ell}=\omega}^{n}\mathds{1}\Big(\lVert X_{\ell}-X_{i}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{j}-X_{i}\rVert\Big)=M.

With a little abuse of notation, let
\epsilon_{i}=Y_{i}-\mu_{D_{i}}(X_{i})
for any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
. Checking the definition of
\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}
in (
5.2
), we decompose
\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}
to be

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{M}^{\rm bc}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{\rm reg}+\frac{1}{n}\Big[\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}-\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\widehat{R}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{\mu}_{1}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{0}(X_{i})\Big]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2D_{i}-1)\Big(1+\frac{K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\Big(Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{D_{i}}(X_{i})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big[\mu_{1}(X_{i})-\mu_{0}(X_{i})\Big]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2D_{i}-1)\Big(1+\frac{K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)\epsilon_{i}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2D_{i}-1)\Big[\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\Big(\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})-\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2D_{i}-1)\Big[\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\Big(\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})+E_{M}+B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}.

We have the following central limit theorem on
\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})+E_{M}
.

Lemma A.1
.

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\sigma^{-1/2}\Big(\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})+E_{M}-\tau\Big)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}N\Big(0,1\Big).

For the bias term
B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}
, define

U_{m,i}:=X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)}-X_{i}~~\text{ for any }i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket\text{ and }m\in\llbracket M\rrbracket.

We then have the following lemma bounding the moments of
U_{M,i}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
p
be any positive integer. Then there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
only depending on
p
such that for any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lVert U_{M,i}\Big\rVert^{p}\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big]\leq C_{p}\Big(\frac{M}{n_{1-D_{i}}}\Big)^{p/d}.

In light of the smoothness conditions on
\mu_{\omega}
and approximation conditions on
\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}
for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
, we can establish the following lemma using Lemma
A.2
.

Lemma A.3
.

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big(B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}\Big)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}0.

Combining Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
A.3
completes the proof. The proof of the consistency of the variance estimator is the same as Theorem
5.1
(ii)
since only Assumption
5.1
and Assumption
5.4
are needed.
∎

A.3.4
Proof of Lemma
A.1

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

For any
x\in\mathbb{X}
, define
\sigma_{\omega}^{2}(x)={\mathrm{E}}[[Y(\omega)-\mu_{\omega}(X)]^{2}\,|\,X=x]
for
\omega\in\{0,1\}
. Let

V^{\tau}:={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\mu_{1}(X)-\mu_{0}(X)-\tau\Big]^{2}~~~{\rm and}~~~V^{E}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{D_{i}}^{2}(X_{i}).

From the standard central limit theorem, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big(\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})-\tau\Big)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}N\Big(0,V^{\tau}\Big).

(A.12)

Let
E_{M,i}=(2D_{i}-1)(1+K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)/M)\epsilon_{i}
for any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
. Conditional on
\bm{X},\bm{D}
,
[E_{M,i}]_{i=1}^{n}
are independent. Notice that
{\mathrm{E}}[E_{M,i}\,|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}]=0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var[E_{M,i}\,|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}]=nV^{E}
. To apply the Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem
(
Billingsley, 1995
, Theorem 27.2)
, it suffices to verify that: for a given
(\bm{X},\bm{D})
,

\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(E_{M,i}\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(\lvert E_{M,i}\rvert>\delta\sqrt{nV^{E}}\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]\to 0,

for all
\delta>0
.

Let
C_{\sigma}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{X},\omega\in\{0,1\}}\{{\mathrm{E}}[\lvert U_{\omega}\rvert^{2+\kappa}\,|\,X=x]\vee{\mathrm{E}}[U^{2}_{\omega}\,|\,X=x]\}<\infty
. Let
p_{1}:=1+\kappa/2
and
p_{2}
be the constant such that
p_{1}^{-1}+p_{2}^{-1}=1
. From Hölder’s inequality and Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(E_{M,i}\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(\lvert E_{M,i}\rvert>\delta\sqrt{nV^{E}}\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert E_{M,i}\Big\rvert^{2+\kappa}\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]\Big)^{1/p_{1}}\Big({\mathrm{P}}\Big(\lvert E_{M,i}\rvert>\delta\sqrt{nV^{E}}\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big)\Big)^{1/p_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq
