\displaystyle\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert E_{M,i}\Big\rvert^{2+\kappa}\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]\Big)^{1/p_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{\delta^{2}nV^{E}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(E_{M,i}\Big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]\Big)^{1/p_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\sigma}}{nV^{E}}\Big(\frac{1}{\delta^{2}nV^{E}}\Big)^{1/p_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2(1+1/p_{2})}.

Notice that
{\mathrm{E}}[1+K^{D_{i}}_{M}(i)/M]^{2(1+1/p_{2})}<\infty
from Theorem
4.2
. Let
c_{\sigma}=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{X},\omega\in\{0,1\}}{\mathrm{E}}[U^{2}_{\omega}\,|\,X=x]>0
. From the definition of
V^{E}
, we have
V^{E}\geq c_{\sigma}
for almost all
\bm{X},\bm{D}
. Then

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(E_{M,i}\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(\lvert E_{M,i}\rvert>\delta\sqrt{nV^{E}}\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]\Big]=O(n^{-1/p_{2}})=o(1).

We thus obtain

\frac{1}{nV^{E}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(E_{M,i}\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(\lvert E_{M,i}\rvert>\delta\sqrt{nV^{E}}\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X},\bm{D}\Big]=o_{\mathrm{P}}(1).

Employing the Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem then yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(V^{E})^{-1/2}E_{M}=\Big(nV^{E}\Big)^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{M,i}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}N\Big(0,1\Big).

(A.13)

Noticing that
\sqrt{n}\Big(\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})-\tau\Big)
and
\sqrt{n}(V^{E})^{-1/2}E_{M}
are asymptotically independent, leveraging the same argument as made in
Abadie and Imbens, 2006
and then combining (
A.12
) and (
A.13
) reaches

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Big(V^{\tau}+V^{E}\Big)^{-1/2}\Big(\bar{\tau}(\bm{X})+E_{M}-\tau\Big)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}N\Big(0,1\Big).

(A.14)

We decompose
V^{E}
as:

\displaystyle V^{E}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big[\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\Big]\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big[\Big(\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}-\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big]\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i}).

(A.15)

For the first term in (
A.15
), notice that
[(X_{i},D_{i},Y_{i})]_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. and
{\mathrm{E}}[D_{i}(e(X_{i}))^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})],{\mathrm{E}}[(1-D_{i})(1-e(X_{i}))^{-2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i})]<\infty
. Using the weak law of large numbers
(
Durrett, 2019
, Theorem 2.2.14)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big(\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}(X)}{e(X)}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}(X)}{1-e(X)}\Big].

For the second term in (
A.15
), using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big[\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\Big]\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\sigma}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[D_{i}\Big\lvert\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\Big\rvert\Big]=C_{\sigma}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[D_{i}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\Big\rvert\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\sigma}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[D_{i}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}-\frac{1-e(X_{i})}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big]{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(2+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}+\frac{1-e(X_{i})}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big]\Big)^{1/2}\Big]=o(1),

where the last step is due to Theorem
4.2
. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=1}^{n}\Big[\Big(1+\frac{K^{1}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}-\Big(\frac{1}{e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}\Big]\sigma_{1}^{2}(X_{i})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}0.

For the third term in (
A.15
), we can establish in the same way that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1,D_{i}=0}^{n}\Big[\Big(\frac{1}{1-e(X_{i})}\Big)^{2}-\Big(1+\frac{K^{0}_{M}(i)}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big]\sigma_{0}^{2}(X_{i})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}0.

Then from (
A.15
),

\displaystyle V^{E}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}(X)}{e(X)}+\frac{\sigma_{0}^{2}(X)}{1-e(X)}\Big].

Using Slutsky’s lemma
(
van der Vaart, 1998
, Theorem 2.8)
and then the definition of
\sigma^{2}
, we complete the proof by (
A.14
).
∎

A.3.5
Proof of Lemma
A.2

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

From Assumption
4.1
and Assumption
5.1
, let
R:={\rm diam}(\mathbb{X})<\infty
and
f_{L}:=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{X},\omega\in\{0,1\}}f_{\omega}(x)>0
. For any
x\in\mathbb{X}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}
and
u\leq R
, from Assumption
4.1
,

\nu_{\omega}\big(B_{x,u}\cap\mathbb{X}\big)\geq f_{L}\lambda\big(B_{x,u}\cap\mathbb{X}\big)\geq f_{L}a\lambda\big(B_{x,u}\big)=f_{L}aV_{d}u^{d}.

Let
c_{0}=f_{L}aV_{d}
. For any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
,
x\in\mathbb{X}
,
M\leq n_{1-D_{i}}
, if
0\leq u\leq Rn_{1-D_{i}}^{1/d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big\lVert X_{j}-X_{i}\Big\rVert\geq un_{1-D_{i}}^{-1/d}\,\Big|\,\bm{D},X_{i}=x,j=j_{M}^{1-D_{i}}(i)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\rm Bin}\Big(n_{1-D_{i}},\nu_{1-D_{i}}\big(B_{x,un_{1-D_{i}}^{-1/d}}\cap\mathbb{X}\big)\Big)\leq M\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\rm Bin}\Big(n_{1-D_{i}},c_{0}\big(un_{1-D_{i}}^{-1/d}\big)^{d}\Big)\leq M\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\rm Bin}\Big(n_{1-D_{i}},c_{0}u^{d}n_{1-D_{i}}^{-1}\Big)\leq M\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big).

Using the Chernoff bound, if
M<c_{0}u^{d}
, then

{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\rm Bin}\Big(n_{1-D_{i}},c_{0}u^{d}n_{1-D_{i}}^{-1}\Big)\leq M\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(M-c_{0}u^{d}+M\log\Big(\frac{c_{0}u^{d}}{M}\Big)\Big).

Notice that the above upper bound does not depend on
x
. We then obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big\lVert X_{j}-X_{i}\Big\rVert\geq un_{1-D_{i}}^{-1/d}\,\Big|\,\bm{D},j=j_{M}^{1-D_{i}}(i)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\Big(M<c_{0}u^{d}\Big)\exp\Big(M-c_{0}u^{d}+M\log\Big(\frac{c_{0}u^{d}}{M}\Big)\Big)+\mathds{1}\Big(M\geq c_{0}u^{d}\Big).

On the other hand, if
u>Rn_{1-D_{i}}^{1/d}
, then the probability is zero from the definition of
R
. Accordingly, the above bound holds for any
u\geq 0
.

For any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket
, we thus have

\displaystyle n_{1-D_{i}}^{p/d}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lVert U_{M,i}\Big\rVert^{p}\,\Big|\,\bm{D}\Big]=p\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big\lVert X_{j}-X_{i}\Big\rVert\geq un_{1-D_{i}}^{-1/d}\,\Big|\,\bm{D},j=j_{M}^{1-D_{i}}(i)\Big)u^{p-1}{\mathrm{d}}u

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big[\mathds{1}\Big(M<c_{0}u^{d}\Big)\exp\Big(M-c_{0}u^{d}+M\log\Big(\frac{c_{0}u^{d}}{M}\Big)\Big)+\mathds{1}\Big(M\geq c_{0}u^{d}\Big)\Big]u^{p-1}{\mathrm{d}}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pc_{0}^{-p/d}d^{-1}\Big[\int_{M}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}t^{M+\frac{p}{d}-1}e^{-t}{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{M}t^{\frac{p}{d}-1}{\mathrm{d}}t\Big],

(A.16)

where the last step is through taking
t=c_{0}u^{d}
.

For the first term in (
A.16
), from Stirling’s formula and
M\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}t^{M+\frac{p}{d}-1}e^{-t}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}t^{M+\frac{p}{d}-1}e^{-t}{\mathrm{d}}t=\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}\Gamma\Big(M+\frac{p}{d}\Big)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}(M+1)^{\frac{p}{d}-1}\Gamma(M+1)\sim\Big(\frac{e}{M}\Big)^{M}(M+1)^{\frac{p}{d}-1}\sqrt{2\pi M}\Big(\frac{M}{e}\Big)^{M}\sim\sqrt{2\pi}M^{\frac{p}{d}-\frac{1}{2}},

where
\sim
means asymptotic convergence.

For the second term in (
A.16
),

\int_{0}^{M}t^{\frac{p}{d}-1}{\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{d}{p}M^{\frac{p}{d}}.

Combining the above two bounds then complete the proof.
∎

A.3.6
Proof of Lemma
A.3

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

We decompose
B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}
as

\displaystyle\lvert B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2D_{i}-1)\Big[\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\Big(\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})-\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})-\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})+\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})\Big)\Big]\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket,m\in\llbracket M\rrbracket}\Big\lvert\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})-\mu_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})-\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{i})+\widehat{\mu}_{1-D_{i}}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket,m\in\llbracket M\rrbracket,\omega\in\{0,1\}}\Big\lvert\mu_{\omega}(X_{i})-\mu_{\omega}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})-\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{i})+\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})\Big\rvert.

(A.17)

Let
k=\lfloor d/2\rfloor+1
. For any
\omega\in\{0,1\}
, from Taylor expansion to
k
th order,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\mu_{\omega}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})-\mu_{\omega}(X_{i})-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\partial^{t}\mu_{\omega}(X_{i})U^{t}_{m,i}\Big\rvert\leq\max_{t\in\Lambda_{k}}\lVert\partial^{t}\mu_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{k}}\lVert U_{m,i}\rVert^{k}.

(A.18)

In the same way,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{j^{1-D_{i}}_{m}(i)})-\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{i})-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\partial^{t}\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{i})U^{t}_{m,i}\Big\rvert\leq\max_{t\in\Lambda_{k}}\lVert\partial^{t}\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{k}}\lVert U_{m,i}\rVert^{k}.

(A.19)

We also have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}(\partial^{t}\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}(X_{i})-\partial^{t}\mu_{\omega}(X_{i}))U^{t}_{m,i}\Big\rvert\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\max_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\lVert\partial^{t}\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}-\partial^{t}\mu_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{\ell!}\sum_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\lVert U_{m,i}\rVert^{\ell}.

(A.20)

Notice that
\lVert U_{M,i}\rVert=\max_{m\in\llbracket M\rrbracket}\lVert U_{m,i}\rVert
for any
i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket,\omega\in\{0,1\}
. Then for any
\omega\in\{0,1\}
, plugging (
A.18
), (
A.19
), (
A.20
) into (
A.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}\rvert\lesssim(1+O_{\mathrm{P}}(1))\max_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket}\lVert U_{M,i}\rVert^{k}+\max_{\ell\in\llbracket k-1\rrbracket}\max_{t\in\Lambda_{\ell}}\lVert\partial^{t}\widehat{\mu}_{\omega}-\partial^{t}\mu_{\omega}\rVert_{\infty}\max_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket}\lVert U_{M,i}\rVert^{\ell}.

From Lemma
A.2
, all moments of
(n/M)^{p/d}\lVert U_{M,i}\rVert^{p}
are bounded. Then for any positive integer
p
and any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\llbracket n\rrbracket}\lVert U_{M,i}\rVert^{p}=o_{\mathrm{P}}\Big(n^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{M}{n}\Big)^{p/d}\Big).

We then obtain

B_{M}-\widehat{B}_{M}=o_{\mathrm{P}}\Big(n^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{M}{n}\Big)^{k/d}\Big)+\max_{\ell\in\llbracket k-1\rrbracket}o_{\mathrm{P}}\Big(n^{-\gamma_{\ell}}n^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{M}{n}\Big)^{\ell/d}\Big).

The proof is thus complete by noticing the definition of
\gamma
and
M\lesssim n^{\gamma}
.
∎

A.4
Proofs of the results in Section
6

Before starting the proof, we first introduce some necessary notation. Given two samples
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
and
\{Z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
, for any
M\in\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
and
\ell\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
, define
j_{M}(\ell)
to be the index of the
M
-th NN of
Z_{\ell}
in
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
. In other words,
j_{M}(\ell)
is the index of
k\in\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
such that

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\mathds{1}\big(\lVert X_{i}-Z_{\ell}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{k}-Z_{\ell}\rVert\big)=M.

Further define
\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)
to be the set of indices of all
M
-NNs of
Z_{\ell}
in
\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell):=\{j_{m}(\ell):m\in\llbracket M\rrbracket\}.

A.4.1
Proof of Theorem
6.1

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Before starting the proof, we first introduce the following lemma about the truncation level for
f_{1}
to attain the desirable estimation accuracy.

Lemma A.4
.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with

\displaystyle f_{1}(x)\geq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}~~~{\rm and}~~~\Delta(x)\geq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}

(A.21)

we have, for all
N_{0}
sufficiently large,

(i)

\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]-r(x)\Big\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}r(x);

(ii)

\Var\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\,\big|\,\bm{X}\big]\Big]\leq\frac{32}{M}[r(x)]^{2};

(iii)

with probability one,

\displaystyle\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\geq C_{1}r(x),

where
C_{1}>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},d
.

Let
X
be drawn from
\nu_{0}
independent of the data. We then have

D_{\phi}(\nu_{1}\|\nu_{0})=\int\phi(r(x))f_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x={\mathrm{E}}[\phi(r(X))].

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying (
A.21
) in Lemma
A.4
. We then decompose
\widehat{D}_{\phi}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{\phi}-D_{\phi}(\nu_{1}\|\nu_{0})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\phi(\widehat{r}_{M}(x))-{\mathrm{E}}[\phi(r(X))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\notin\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)-{\mathrm{E}}[\phi(r(X))]

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}+T_{5}.

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\widehat{D}_{\phi}-D_{\phi}(\nu_{1}\|\nu_{0})\Big]^{2}\leq 5\Big({\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}]^{2}+{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}]^{2}+{\mathrm{E}}[T_{3}]^{2}+{\mathrm{E}}[T_{4}]^{2}+{\mathrm{E}}[T_{5}]^{2}\Big).

We then consider
{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}]^{2},{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}]^{2},{\mathrm{E}}[T_{3}]^{2},{\mathrm{E}}[T_{4}]^{2},{\mathrm{E}}[T_{5}]^{2}
seperately. We have the following lemmas.

Lemma A.5
.

It holds that

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}]^{2}\leq C\Big[\frac{1}{N_{1}}+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}\Big],

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Lemma A.6
.

It holds that

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}]^{2}\leq C\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}+\Big(\frac{1}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big],

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

Lemma A.7
.

It holds that

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{3}]^{2}\leq C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

Lemma A.8
.

It holds that

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{4}]^{2}\leq C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},a,H,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

Notice that
T_{5}
is a linear combination of the function of i.i.d. random variables with mean zero. From the boundedness assumption on
r
, it is easy to establish

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}[T_{5}]^{2}\leq C\frac{1}{N_{0}},

(A.22)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Combining Lemma
A.5
, Lemma
A.6
, Lemma
A.7
, Lemma
A.8
, and (
A.22
) completes the proof.
∎

A.4.2
Proof of Lemma
A.4

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

For Part (i), recall that in the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
, we take
\delta_{N}=(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
. Take
C_{0}>L(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}
. Then
f_{1}(x)\geq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}>L\delta_{N}
. Take
C_{0}>2(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}
. Then
\Delta(x)>2\delta_{N}
. Then for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
, we have
B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert}\subset S_{1}
. We take
N_{0}
large enough so that
\delta_{N}<f_{L}/(4L)
. Then from the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
,
W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}
implies
\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
. From Case I in the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
, we use (
7.31
) and (
7.32
), and take
N_{0}
large enough so that
L\delta_{N}\leq f_{U}\wedge(f_{L}/4)
. We then have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{r}_{M}(x)\big]-r(x)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Big\lvert\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}-\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big\rvert\bigvee\Big\lvert\frac{f_{1}(x)-L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)+2L\delta_{N}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{0}+1}-\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\Big\rvert+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{f_{0}(x)L\delta_{N}+2f_{1}(x)L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)(f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N})}+\frac{1}{N_{0}+1}\frac{f_{1}(x)+L\delta_{N}}{f_{0}(x)-2L\delta_{N}}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{f_{L}}L\delta_{N}+r(x)\frac{4}{f_{L}}L\delta_{N}+\frac{4f_{U}}{f_{L}}\frac{1}{N_{0}+1}+o(N_{0}^{-\gamma}).

Taking
C_{0}>L\frac{4}{f_{L}}(\frac{4}{f_{L}V_{d}})^{1/d}
, Part (i) then holds when
N_{0}
is sufficiently large.

For Part (ii), we take
C_{0}
large enough as in Part (i). Then
W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}
implies
\lVert Z-x\rVert\leq\delta_{N}
. For any
\epsilon>0
, we take
C_{0}
and
N_{0}
sufficiently large such that for any
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq 2\delta_{N}
, we have
\lvert f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
and
\lvert f_{0}(z)-f_{0}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{L}
, which is achievable from the Lipschitz condition. Then
\lvert f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{1}(x)
and
\lvert f_{0}(z)-f_{0}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x)
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
f_{1}(x)\geq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
and
\Delta(x)\geq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}
, for
N_{0}
sufficiently large, we have for all
0\leq w\leq w+t\leq 2M/N_{0}
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(w\leq W\leq w+t\Big)=\nu_{1}\Big(\Big\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})\in[w,w+t]\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(x)(1+\epsilon)}{f_{0}(x)(1-\epsilon)}\nu_{0}\Big(\Big\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})\in[w,w+t]\Big\}\Big),

and then

\limsup_{t\to 0}t^{-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(w\leq W\leq w+t\Big)\leq r(x)\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}(1+\epsilon).

Proceeding in the same way as the proof of Theorem
4.3
2
and Lemma
7.4
, we prove Part (ii).

On Part (iii), we cite a result on lower bounding the
M
-NN distance without proof.

Lemma A.9
.

(
Biau and Devroye, 2015
, Theorem 4.1)

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{N_{0}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be
N_{0}
independent copies from a probability measure with density
f_{0}
. Assume that
f_{0}
is uniformly bounded by a universal constant
f_{U}
. If
M/\log N_{0}\to\infty
as
N_{0}\to\infty
, then with probability one, for all
N_{0}
large enough,

\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(x)-x\Big\rVert\geq C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{U}
and
d
.

Notice that for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, if
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(x)-x\Big\rVert
, then
z\in A_{M}(x)
. From Lemma
A.9
, with probability one, for all
N_{0}
sufficiently large, we have

\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\geq\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(B_{x,\lVert\mathcal{X}_{(M)}(x)-x\rVert/2}\Big)\geq\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(B_{x,C(M/N_{0})^{1/d}/2}\Big),

where
C>0
is the constant in Lemma
A.9
. Taking
C_{0}
large enough so that
C_{0}>C/2
and
C_{0}\geq LC
, then
\Delta(x)>C(M/N_{0})^{1/d}/2
and for any
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq C(M/N_{0})^{1/d}/2
, we have
f_{1}(z)\geq\frac{1}{2}f_{1}(x)
. Then

\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(x)\big)\geq\frac{N_{0}}{M}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{2}V_{d}\Big(\frac{C}{2}\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{d}}\Big)^{d}=2^{-(d+1)}C^{d}V_{d}f_{1}(x)\geq 2^{-(d+1)}C^{d}V_{d}f_{L}r(x).

We then complete the proof of Part (iii).
∎

A.4.3
Proof of Lemma
A.5

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

Notice that

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}]^{2}={\mathrm{E}}\Big[{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}^{2}\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big]={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big({\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)^{2}+\Var[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big].

We consider the above two terms separately.

Step I.

{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]
.

Let
g(x)
be a given twice continuously differentiable function on
[0,\infty)
. Equation 2.5 in
Totik, 1994
gives

\displaystyle\lvert g(t)-g(x)-g^{\prime}(x)(t-x)\rvert\leq 4\frac{(t-x)^{2}}{x}\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty},

(A.23)

for all
x,t\in[0,\infty)
.

From (
A.23
),

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert

\displaystyle=\Big\lvert\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+\Big\lvert\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[g\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-g\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]}\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\frac{\Var[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]}{{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]}.

From the definition of
\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})
,

\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}}\frac{K_{M}(i)}{M}=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(Z_{j}\in A_{M}(i)\big).

Then conditional on
\bm{X}
,

\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\sim{\rm Bin}(N_{1},A_{M}(i)).

It is easy to calculate

{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]=\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big),~~\Var[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]=\frac{N_{0}^{2}}{N_{1}M^{2}}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\big[1-\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\big].

Then

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\big[1-\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}.

We then cite the equivalence theorem.

Lemma A.10
.

(
Ditzian and Totik, 2012
, Theorem 2.1.1)

For any real-valued function
\phi
on
[0,\infty)
and
t>0
, there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that

\inf_{g\in C^{2}[0,\infty)}\Big(\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+t^{2}\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\Big)\leq C\omega_{\varphi}^{2}(\phi,t)_{\infty},

where
\omega_{\varphi}^{2}(\phi,t)_{\infty}
is the second-order modulus of smoothness with
\varphi(x)=\sqrt{x}
, and
C^{2}[0,\infty)
is the class of all twice continuously differentiable functions on
[0,\infty)
.

From Example 3.4.2 in
Ditzian and Totik, 2012
, when
\phi(x)=x\log x
, we have
\omega_{\varphi}^{2}(\phi,t)_{\infty}\asymp t^{2}
. Then from Lemma
A.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert\lesssim\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}.

(A.24)

Step II.

\Var[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]
.

Conditional on
\bm{X}
,

\displaystyle\Var[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]

\displaystyle=\Var\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\Big]\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Var\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big].

(A.25)

We then apply Efron-Stein inequality
(
Boucheron et al., 2013
, Theorem 3.1)
. Recall
\bm{Z}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N_{1}})
and for any
\ell\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
, define

\bm{Z}_{\ell}:=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\ell-1},{\widetilde{Z}}_{\ell},Z_{\ell+1},\ldots,Z_{N_{1}}),

where
[{\widetilde{Z}}_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{1}}
are independent copies of
[Z_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{1}}
.

Fix
\ell\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
. Recall that
\mathcal{J}_{M}(\cdot)
are defined based on
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Z}
. We then define
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\cdot)
in the same way but based on
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Z}_{\ell}
.

Let
\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}
be the density ratio estimator based on
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Z}_{\ell}
. Then for any
i\in\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
,

\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(j)\big),~~\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(i\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(j)\big).

Notice that for
j\in\llbracket N_{1}\rrbracket
and
j\neq\ell
,
\mathcal{J}_{M}(j)=\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(j)
since both
\mathcal{J}_{M}(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\cdot)
are based on
\bm{X}
. Then if
i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)
or
i\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)
, we have
\lvert\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})-\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\rvert=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}
. If
i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\cap\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)
or
i\notin\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\cup\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)
, we have
\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})=\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})
. As a result,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big(\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\in(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\cup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\end{subarray}}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\cup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\in(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\cup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\end{subarray}}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2M}{N_{0}^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\in(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\cup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\end{subarray}}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big],

where for any set
A
,
\lvert A\rvert
is the cardinality of
A
. The last two steps are due to the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the fact that
\lvert\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\rvert\leq M,\lvert\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\rvert\leq M
. Thus

\lvert(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\bigcup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\rvert\leq 2M.

Since
[X_{i}]_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
i.i.d., we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big(\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(i\in(\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell))\cup(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell))\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\Big[\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\Big)+\mathds{1}\Big(\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\Big],

(A.26)

where the last step is from symmetry.

Let

\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})=\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\sum_{j=1,j\neq\ell}^{N_{1}}\mathds{1}\big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(j)\big).

Then
\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})=\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\mathds{1}\big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\big),\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})=\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\mathds{1}\big(i\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\big)
. Notice that
\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})
is a function of
\bm{X}
and
[Z_{j}]_{j=1,j\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}
. Since
[Z_{j}]_{j=1}^{N_{1}}
are i.i.d. and independent with
{\widetilde{Z}}_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\cdot{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big].

(A.27)

For the second term in (
A.27
),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\setminus\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\leq{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}\Big(i\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(\ell)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]=\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big).

(A.28)

For the first term in (
A.27
), notice that
\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\sim{\rm Bin}\Big(N_{1}-1,\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)
. Then using the Chernoff bound, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\leq\frac{1}{2}(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(1-\log 2)(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\geq 2(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-(2\log 2-1)(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big).

If
\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\geq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}-1}
, then for any
\gamma>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\gamma}>0
only depending on
\gamma
such that, with probability at least
1-N_{1}^{-\gamma}
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N_{1}}\Big)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\leq 2\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N_{1}}\Big)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big).

Notice that
\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\prec\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)
when
\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\geq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}-1}
. Then for
N_{0}
sufficiently large, with probability at least
1-N_{1}^{-\gamma}
, the following sandwich inequality holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\leq\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\leq 4\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big).

(A.29)

If
\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}-1}
, then by the Chernoff bound,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{N_{1}M}{N_{0}}\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\geq 2C_{\gamma}\log N_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(2C_{\gamma}\log N_{1}-(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)-2C_{\gamma}\log N_{1}\log\Big(\frac{2C_{\gamma}\log N_{1}}{(N_{1}-1)\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-(2\log 2-1)C_{\gamma}\log N_{1}\Big)\leq N_{1}^{-\gamma}.

Then for
N_{0}
sufficiently large, with probability at least
1-N_{1}^{-\gamma}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\leq 2C_{\gamma}\frac{N_{0}\log N_{1}}{N_{1}M}.

(A.30)

Then for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2(1-\epsilon)}\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big)+\frac{2}{N_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big(2\frac{N_{0}}{M}\log\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+C\Big[1+\Big(\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}\Big]\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\geq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big),

(A.31)

where
C_{\epsilon}>0
is a constant only depending on
\epsilon
and
C>0
is a universal constant. The first term is due to (
A.30
) and the inequality

\lvert x\log x-y\log y\lvert\leq C_{\epsilon}\lvert x-y\rvert^{1-\epsilon},

whenever
x,y\in[0,1]
and
\lvert x-y\rvert\leq\frac{1}{2}
. The second term is from
\phi(x)=x\log x
and
\widehat{r}^{-\ell}_{M}(X_{i})+\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\leq\frac{N_{0}}{M}
. The third term is from
\phi^{\prime}(x)=1+\log x
and the mean value theorem by using the sandwich inequality (
A.29
).

Plugging (
A.28
) and (
A.31
) into (
A.26
) and tracing back to (
A.27
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big(\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[C_{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2(1-\epsilon)}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big)+\frac{2}{N_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big(2\frac{N_{0}}{M}\log\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big[1+\Big(\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}\Big]\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\geq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big)\Big].

(A.32)

For the first term in (
A.32
), since
MN_{1}/(N_{0}\log^{2}N_{1})\to\infty
,

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2(1-\epsilon)}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}C_{\gamma}\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2(1-\epsilon)}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}=C_{\epsilon}C_{\gamma}\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{1-2\epsilon}\log N_{1}\prec\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}},

(A.33)

where we take
\epsilon
sufficiently small.

For the second term in (
A.32
), using Corollary
4.1
,
{\mathrm{E}}[r(X)]=1
and
N_{1}^{-\frac{d}{1+d}}\log N_{0}\to 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{M}{N_{0}}\frac{1}{N_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\log\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big(\log\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{1}^{\gamma}}\Big(\log\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\prec\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}},

(A.34)

where we take
\gamma
sufficiently large.

For the third term in (
A.32
), using Corollary
4.1
,

\displaystyle\frac{M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[1+\Big(\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}\Big]\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\geq C_{\gamma}\frac{\log N_{1}}{N_{1}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[1+\Big(\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}\Big]\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{N_{1}M}\Big)^{2}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)+\Big(\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big)^{2}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\Big(A_{M}(i)\Big)\Big)^{2}\vee 4e^{-2}\Big]\lesssim C\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}},

(A.35)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
. The last step is from
(\log x)^{2}\leq x
when
x\geq 1
and the maximum of
(\log x)^{2}x
is
4e^{-2}
when
x\in[0,1]
.

Plugging (
A.33
), (
A.34
), (
A.35
) into (
A.32
) yields

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big(\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C\frac{1}{N_{1}^{2}},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

By Efron-Stein inequality and (
A.25
),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var[T_{1}\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Var\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big(\phi\Big(\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)-\phi\Big(\widehat{r}^{\prime}_{M}(X_{i})\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C\frac{1}{N_{1}},

(A.36)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Combining (
A.24
) and (
A.36
) completes the proof.
∎

A.4.4
Proof of Lemma
A.6

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

From (
A.23
),

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert

\displaystyle=\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[g\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-g\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]-{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}])^{2}}{{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{\Var[{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\,|\,X_{i}]}{{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big].

Using Lemma
A.4
,

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{\frac{32}{M}[r(X_{i})]^{2}}{\frac{1}{2}r(X_{i})}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+4\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\frac{64}{M}{\mathrm{E}}[r(X_{i})]

\displaystyle=2\lVert\phi-g\rVert_{\infty}+\frac{256}{M}\lVert xg^{\prime\prime}(x)\rVert_{\infty}.

Using Lemma
A.10
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[T_{2}\,|\,\bm{X}]\rvert\lesssim C\frac{1}{M},

(A.37)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

For the variance, we have

\Var[T_{2}]=\Var\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big].

To apply Efron-Stein inequality, recall
\bm{X}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{N_{0}})
and for any
\ell\in\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
,

\bm{X}_{\ell}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{\ell-1},{\widetilde{X}}_{\ell},X_{\ell+1},\ldots,X_{N_{0}}),

where
[{\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{0}}
are independent copies of
[X_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{0}}
.

Fix
\ell\in\llbracket N_{0}\rrbracket
. Recall that
A_{M}(\cdot)
are defined based on
\bm{X}
. We then define
A^{\prime}_{M}(\cdot)
in the same way but based on
\bm{X}_{\ell}
, and define
A^{\ell}_{M}(\cdot)
based on
(X_{1},\ldots,X_{\ell-1},X_{\ell+1},\ldots,X_{N_{0}})
.

(1) If
i=\ell
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,X_{\ell}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}_{\ell}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell})\,|\,{\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,X_{\ell}]\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}_{\ell}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell})\,|\,{\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}]\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big({\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big).

(2) If
i\neq\ell
,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle-\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}_{\ell}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}_{\ell}]\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\prime}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}^{\ell}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\prime}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big).

Since
[X_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{0}}
and
[{\widetilde{X}}_{\ell}]_{\ell=1}^{N_{0}}
are exchangeable, using Efron-Stein inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Var[T_{2}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,X_{\ell}]\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)+\phi^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,X_{\ell}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}.

For the first term, from Corollary
4.1
,
r(x)
is bounded and
\phi^{2}(x)\leq x^{3}
when
x\geq 1
, we obtain

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)+\phi^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{\ell})\,|\,X_{\ell}]\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{\ell}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]\lesssim C,

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

For the second term, notice that
A_{M}(i)\subset A_{M}^{\ell}(i)
and

A_{M}^{\ell}(i)\setminus A_{M}(i)=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:j_{M+1}(z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(z)\}.

Then for an independent copy
Z\sim\nu_{1}
,

\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)-\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)=\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\leq\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big).

By the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)-\phi\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert 1+\log\xi_{i}\Big\rvert\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big),

where
\xi_{i}
is between
\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)
and
\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)
.

When
\xi_{i}\leq 1
,
\lvert\log\xi_{i}\rvert\leq-\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq 1\Big)
. When
\xi_{i}\geq 1
,
\lvert\log\xi_{i}\rvert\leq\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A^{\ell}_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big)
. Then

\Big\lvert 1+\log\xi_{i}\Big\rvert\leq 1-\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq 1\Big)+\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big).

Notice that events
\{j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\}
are disjoint for
i\neq\ell
. We then have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big[1+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big[1+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[1+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[1+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big).

From Corollary
4.1
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\Big[1+\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C,

(A.38)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Let
\Xi_{\ell}\,|\,\bm{X}
follows the same distribution as
Z\,|\,\bm{X},\{Z\in A_{M}(\ell)\}
for
Z\sim\nu_{1}
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\mathds{1}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(Z))\big)\mathds{1}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(Z))\big)\mathds{1}\Big(\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(\Xi_{\ell}))\big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)

Then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{4}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(Z))\big)\mathds{1}\Big(\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\Big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{4}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(\Xi_{\ell}))\big)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big]\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{4}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(j_{M+1}(\Xi_{\ell}))\big)\Big]^{4}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]^{4}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{4}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big]^{4}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]^{4}\Big)^{1/2}

for some
i\neq\ell
. The last step is due to the fact that
[X_{i}]_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}
are exchangeable and
\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)
implies
j_{M+1}(Z)\neq\ell
.

By Corollary
4.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C,

(A.39)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U}
.

Using the same trick, by Lemma
A.4
and Corollary
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,i\neq\ell}^{N_{0}}\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq 1\Big)\Big[\frac{N_{0}}{M}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(j_{M+1}(Z)=i,\ell\in\mathcal{J}_{M}(Z)\,\Big|\,\bm{X}\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq 1\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}^{4}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(C_{1}r(X_{i})\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\nu_{1}\big(A_{M}(i)\big)\leq 1\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}^{4}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(C_{1}r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\nu_{1}^{4}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(C_{1}r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{4}\nu_{1}^{4}\big(A_{M}(\ell)\big)\Big]\Big]^{1/2}\lesssim C,

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.
∎

A.4.5
Proof of Lemma
A.7

Proof of Lemma
A.7
.

We have

\displaystyle\lvert T_{3}\rvert

\displaystyle=\Big\lvert\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big).

From Lemma
A.4
, if
X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}
, then for
N_{0}
sufficiently large,

\frac{1}{2}r(X_{i})\leq{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\leq\frac{3}{2}r(X_{i}).

From the mean value theorem and the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)
,

\displaystyle\lvert T_{3}\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[1+\Big\lvert\log r(X_{i})\Big\rvert\Big]\Big\lvert{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]-r(X_{i})\Big\rvert\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d}\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[1+\Big\lvert\log r(X_{i})\Big\rvert\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big),

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}[\lvert T_{3}\rvert]\leq C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d},

(A.40)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

From the i.i.d.-ness of
[X_{i}]_{i=1}^{N_{0}}
,

\displaystyle\Var[T_{3}]

\displaystyle=\Var\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{0}}\Var\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,X_{i}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{0}}C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

(A.41)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
. The last step is due to the mean value theorem.

Combining (
A.40
) and (
A.41
) completes the proof.
∎

A.4.6
Proof of Lemma
A.8

Proof of Lemma
A.8
.

We seperate
S_{0}\setminus\mathcal{E}
into three sets
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2},\mathcal{E}_{3}
, where
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\{x\in S_{0}:0<f_{1}(x)\leq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}\}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:f_{1}(x)=0,\Delta(x)>\delta_{N}^{\prime}\}
,
\mathcal{E}_{3}=\{x\in S_{0}:f_{1}(x)=0,\Delta(x)\leq\delta_{N}^{\prime}\}\cup\{x\in S_{0}:f_{1}(x)>C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d},\Delta(x)\leq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}\}
, where
C_{0}
is the constant in Lemma
A.4
, and
\delta_{N}^{\prime}
is defined in the proof of Theorem
4.4
.

For
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, from the Cauchy–Schwarz inquality,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]

We take
N_{0}
sufficiently large such that
C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}\leq f_{L}e^{-1}\wedge 1
. Then for any
x\in\mathcal{E}_{1}
,
r(x)\leq e^{-1}
and
f_{1}(x)\leq 1
.

If
{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\leq r(X_{i})
, then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\phi^{2}\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]\leq\phi^{2}\Big(f_{L}^{-1}C_{0}\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{1/d}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big(0<f_{1}(X_{i})\leq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d}\log^{2}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big(0<f_{1}(X_{i})\leq C_{0}(\frac{M}{N_{0}})^{1/d}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d}\log^{2}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big(\Big\lvert\log f_{1}(X_{i})\Big\rvert\geq\Big\lvert\frac{1}{d}\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)-\log C_{0}\Big\rvert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d}\log^{2}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{d}\log\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)-\log C_{0}\Big)^{-4}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(f_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d
.

If
{\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]>r(X_{i})
, then from mean value theorem,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(1+\log\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big)^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]-r(X_{i})\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big(1+\log\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big)^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d}\Big(1+{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\log^{4}\Big(f_{1}(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\Big)\lesssim C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

We then obtain

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{1}\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},L,d,f_{U}^{\prime}
.

For
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, notice that
(\log x)^{2}<\frac{1}{x}+x
. In the same way as case II of the proof of Theorem
4.4
, for any
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\phi^{2}\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{2}\Big)\Big]\prec N_{0}^{-\gamma}.

For
\mathcal{E}_{3}
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{0}}{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]^{2}\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big)\Big]+\Big(1-\frac{1}{N_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{j})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{j})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big)\mathds{1}\Big(X_{j}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big)\Big].

Notice that

\Big\lvert\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)\Big\rvert+\Big\lvert\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big\rvert\leq\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)^{2}+r(X_{i})^{2}+2.

Employing the same approach as in the proof of case III in Theorem
4.4
, for any
X_{i},X_{j}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}
, one can establish

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)^{4}\,\Big|\,X_{i},X_{j}\Big]\lesssim C,

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},a
.

Then from the independence of
X_{i}
and
X_{j}
,

{\mathrm{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{0}}\Big[\phi\Big({\mathrm{E}}[\widehat{r}_{M}(X_{i})\,|\,\bm{X}]\Big)-\phi\Big(r(X_{i})\Big)\Big]\mathds{1}\Big(X_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{3}\Big)\Big]^{2}\lesssim C\Big(\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)^{2/d},

where the constant
C>0
only depending on
f_{L},f_{U},a,H,d
.
∎

A.4.7
Proof of Proposition
6.1

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

We take
\nu_{0}
to be the uniform distribution of an arbitrary support such that the density is
f_{L}
. Notice that in this case, the KL divergence estimation is reduced to the estimation of differential entropy with
N_{1}
samples. We then complete the proof using the minimax lower bound in bounded support Lipschitz entropy estimation without the assumption that the density is bounded away from zero
(
Han et al., 2020
, Theorem 7)
.
∎

A.5
Proofs of results in Section
7

A.5.1
Proof of Lemma
7.1

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

The first inequality is directly from the definition of Lebesgue points. The second inequality follows by

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\frac{\nu(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}-f(x)\Big\rvert\leq\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f(y)-f(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f(y)-f(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y=\frac{\lambda(B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert})}{\lambda(B_{z,\lVert z-x\rVert})}\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f(y)-f(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{d}\frac{1}{\lambda(B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert})}\int_{B_{x,2\lVert z-x\rVert}}\lvert f(y)-f(x)\rvert{\mathrm{d}}y,

and then the definition of Lebesgue points.
∎

A.5.2
Proof of Lemma
7.3

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

Fix any
(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{x,\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},L,d,\delta)
.

We first prove the first claim. First consider
f_{1}(x)>0
. For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\delta^{\prime}>0
such that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta^{\prime}
, we have
\lvert f_{0}(z)-f_{0}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{0}(x)
and
\lvert f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon f_{1}(x)
from the local Lipschitz assumption. We take
w>0
sufficiently small such that
w<(1-\epsilon)f_{0}(x)\lambda(B_{0,\delta^{\prime}})
. Then
W\leq w
implies
\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\delta^{\prime}
. Then for
w>0
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(W\leq w)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq w,\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\delta^{\prime}\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq w\Big)=\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}w,

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}(W\leq w)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq w,\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\delta^{\prime}\Big)\geq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq w,\lVert x-Z\rVert\leq\delta\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{0}(x)}{f_{1}(x)}\nu_{1}(B_{x,\lVert x-Z\rVert})\leq w\Big)=\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}w.

Then we have

\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}\leq\liminf_{w\to 0}w^{-1}{\mathrm{P}}(W\leq w)\leq\limsup_{w\to 0}w^{-1}{\mathrm{P}}(W\leq w)\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}.

Since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, we obtain

f_{W}(0)=\lim_{w\to 0}w^{-1}{\mathrm{P}}(W\leq w)=\frac{f_{1}(x)}{f_{0}(x)}=r(x).

The case for
f_{1}(x)=0
can be established in the same way. This completes the proof of the first claim.

For the second claim, for any
0<\epsilon<f_{L}
, there exists
\delta^{\prime}>0
such that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying
\lVert z-x\rVert\leq\delta^{\prime}
, we have
\lvert f_{0}(z)-f_{0}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon
and
\lvert f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(x)\rvert\leq\epsilon
from the local Lipschitz assumption. We take
N_{0}
sufficiently large such that
2\frac{M}{N_{0}}<(f_{L}-\epsilon)\lambda(B_{0,\delta^{\prime}})
. Then for any
0<w\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}
, we have
w<(f_{L}-\epsilon)\lambda(B_{0,\delta^{\prime}})
. We take
t>0
such that
w+t<(f_{L}-\epsilon)\lambda(B_{0,\delta^{\prime}})
. Then for any
(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{x,\rm p}(f_{L},f_{U},L,d,\delta)
,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(w\leq W\leq w+t\Big)=\nu_{1}\Big(\Big\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})\in[w,w+t]\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(x)+\epsilon}{f_{0}(x)-\epsilon}\nu_{0}\Big(\Big\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\nu_{0}(B_{z,\lVert x-z\rVert})\in[w,w+t]\Big\}\Big).

Notice that
f_{0}
is lower bounded by
f_{L}
. Then for
N_{0}
sufficiently large,

\limsup_{t\to 0}t^{-1}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(w\leq W\leq w+t\Big)\leq\frac{f_{1}(x)+\epsilon}{f_{0}(x)-\epsilon}(1+\epsilon).

This then completes the proof.
∎

A.5.3
Proof of Lemma
7.4

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

Due to the i.i.d.-ness of
Z
and
{\widetilde{Z}}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{2\frac{M}{N_{0}}}\int_{0}^{2\frac{M}{N_{0}}}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq w_{1},{\widetilde{V}}\geq w_{2}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq w_{1}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq w_{2}\Big)\Big]f_{W}(w_{1})f_{W}(w_{2}){\mathrm{d}}w_{1}{\mathrm{d}}w_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{2\frac{M}{N_{0}}}\int_{0}^{2\frac{M}{N_{0}}}\Big\lvert{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq w_{1},{\widetilde{V}}\geq w_{2}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq w_{1}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq w_{2}\Big)\Big\rvert{\mathrm{d}}w_{1}{\mathrm{d}}w_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}\Big\lvert{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\Big\rvert{\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2},

where the last step is from taking
w_{1}=\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})
and
w_{2}=\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})
.

Let

S(t_{1},t_{2}):=\Big\lvert{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\Big\rvert.

If
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big).

If
t_{2}\geq t_{1}\geq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big).

Then for
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}\vee t_{2})\Big).

If
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq 0
and
{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\geq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)
,

\displaystyle S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big).

If
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq 0
and
{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)
,

\displaystyle S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}),{\widetilde{V}}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big)\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(V\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big)={\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big).

If
t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq 0
, we can establish in the same way that

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big).

Then for
t_{1},t_{2}\leq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}\wedge t_{2})\Big).

For
t_{1}\geq 0\geq t_{2}
, if
t_{1}+t_{2}\geq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big),

and if
t_{1}+t_{2}\leq 0
,

S(t_{1},t_{2})\leq{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big).

Then

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{-1}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}+\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{0}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\Big[\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}+\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}+2\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\Big],

(A.42)

where the last step is from the symmetry of
S(t_{1},t_{2})
.

For the first term in (
A.42
), by the symmetry of
S(t_{1},t_{2})
and the Chernoff bound,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}=2\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}S(t_{1},t_{2})\mathds{1}(t_{1}\geq t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}\vee t_{2})\Big)\mathds{1}(t_{1}\geq t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}=2\int_{0}^{\infty}t{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{\infty}t(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{M+1}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t-\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{M}\Big(-1-(M+1)\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)-\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}+\frac{1}{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{1}{M}+\frac{e^{M}}{M}\int_{1}^{\infty}t^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\frac{1}{M}+\frac{e^{M}}{M}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{M}e^{-Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}+\frac{e^{M}}{M}\frac{1}{M^{M+1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{M}e^{-t}{\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{1}{M}+\frac{e^{M}}{M^{M+2}}\Gamma(M+1)=\frac{1}{M}+\frac{e^{M}}{M^{M+2}}\sqrt{2\pi M}\Big(\frac{M}{e}\Big)^{M}(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}(1+o(1)),

(A.43)

where the second last inequality from Stirling’s approximation since
M\to\infty
. We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\leq\frac{2}{M}(1+o(1)).

(A.44)

For the second term in (
A.42
),

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}=2\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2})\mathds{1}(t_{1}\leq t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\leq 2\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2})\mathds{1}(t_{1}\leq t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1}\wedge t_{2})\Big)\mathds{1}(t_{1}\leq t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}=2\int_{-1}^{0}(-t){\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{1}t{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1-t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t\leq 2\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)^{M}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t.

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t(1-t)^{M}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t=-\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{M+1}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{M}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{M}\Big(-1+(M+1)\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{M}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t\Big)+\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{M}e^{Mt}{\mathrm{d}}t\leq\frac{1}{M}.

(A.45)

We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{0}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\leq\frac{2}{M}.

(A.46)

For the third term in (
A.42
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-t_{1}}^{0}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{1})\Big){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-1}^{-t_{1}}{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t_{2})\Big){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{-1}^{0}(-t){\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t{\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\geq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t+\int_{-1}^{0}(-t){\mathrm{P}}\Big(U_{(M)}\leq\frac{M}{N_{0}}(1+t)\Big){\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}(1+o(1))+\frac{1}{M}=\frac{2}{M}(1+o(1)),

where the last step is from (
A.43
) and (
A.45
).

We then obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-1}^{0}S(t_{1},t_{2}){\mathrm{d}}t_{1}{\mathrm{d}}t_{2}\leq\frac{2}{M}(1+o(1)).

(A.47)

Plugging (
A.44
), (
A.46
), (
A.47
) into (
A.42
) yields

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{N_{0}}{M}\Big)^{2}\Big[{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,{\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)-{\mathrm{P}}\Big(W\leq V,W\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big){\mathrm{P}}\Big({\widetilde{W}}\leq{\widetilde{V}},{\widetilde{W}}\leq 2\frac{M}{N_{0}}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32\Big(\frac{f_{U}}{f_{L}}\Big)^{2}\frac{1}{M}(1+o(1)),

(A.48)

and thus completes the proof.
∎
